Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. But I’ve been more tense and a little down, and I keep dropping small things I normally care about, like fair planning notes or design drafts. Yeah… a little, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still getting through the basic stuff.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Then I feel guilty and sometimes cancel plans instead of just catching up. I’m still doing the basics, though… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Then I feel guilty, and sometimes I cancel plans instead of catching up.\n\nI know that probably sounds a little silly… but that’s more what it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts or worries that keep coming back to you, even when you try to focus on other things?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in a big way, I don’t think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're on edge?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. Not constantly, but more than I’d like.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble sleeping due to worrying or feeling tense?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often.” I mean, I have nights where I’m tense and it takes me a bit to settle down, but I don’t know if it’s enough to call it a real sleep problem.\n\nI can usually sleep eventually. I don’t want to exaggerate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I’m still doing the basic errands and things I have to do, so I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nBut I have noticed I’m not as reliable with the volunteer and creative stuff people usually count on me for… like fair planning, or following through on design things. I think it’s more tied to the arts board expectations, yeah. Not just worrying in general.

It’s mostly about things coming up with the arts board stuff and trying to keep my image and routine together.\n\nI’ll think, like, “What if I fall behind again?” or “What if I mess up something small?” And then I get tense, and I end up dropping little things I normally care about, like fair planning notes or design drafts. Then I feel guilty and sometimes cancel plans instead of just catching up. I’m still doing the basics, though… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not in the way you mean, I don’t think.\n\nI’ll have worries come back up, like replaying whether I handled something okay, but it’s not like a thought that won’t leave me alone all day or anything. At least I haven’t noticed it that strongly. Is that what you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain situations because you fear something might happen?\n\nPatient: I think… sort of, but not because I’m scared something terrible will happen. It’s more like I worry I’ll fall behind or look like I don’t have it together.\n\nSince I’ve been trying to clean up my image and routine for the arts board stuff, I’ve been more tense and kind of down than I expected. I’m still doing the basics, but I’ll drop smaller things I normally care about, like fair planning notes or design drafts. Then I feel guilty, and sometimes I cancel plans instead of catching up.\n\nI know that probably sounds a little silly… but that’s more what it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts or worries that keep coming back to you, even when you try to focus on other things?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in a big way, I don’t think. Kind of down too. I’ll be trying to keep up, and then I end up dropping smaller things I usually care about, like fair planning notes or design drafts, and then I feel guilty and sometimes cancel plans instead of just catching up.\n\nSo… on edge, yes, maybe. I don’t want to exaggerate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I’m still doing the basic errands and things I have to do, so I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nBut I have noticed I’m not as reliable with the volunteer and creative stuff people usually count on me for… like fair planning, or following through on design things. I’ll mean to do it, and then I get caught up worrying or second-guessing, and it slips. So… I guess it is interfering some.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is significantly affecting your ability to function in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: I think… not significantly in the sense that I’m still managing the basic errands and commitments. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function.\n\nBut it has affected some things. I’ve been less dependable with the volunteer and creative roles people usually count on me for, like the fair planning stuff and following through on design things. I just don’t want to overstate it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant life changes or stressors recently, aside from the arts board issues?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, not that I can think of. But I’m not sure if I’m interpreting the question the way you mean it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your tension and guilt are more related to the pressure of meeting expectations for the arts board, or are they more about worrying in general?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more tied to the arts board expectations, yeah.
